text(await tools.exec_command({cmd:"@'\nfrom pathlib import Path\nr=Path(r'C:\\Users\\Joshu\\Documents\\Ai-os')\nfor name in ['CONTEXT.md','PLAN.md','PLAN-REVIEW-LOG.md','No file.md','Untitled.md','Untitled 2.md','Untitled 3.md','Untitled 4.md','Untitled.base','Untitled 1.base']:\n print('\\nFILE '+name+'\\n'+(r/name).read_text(encoding='utf-8'))\n'@ | python -",max_output_tokens:11500}));


## Tool: custom_tool_call_output

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 0.5 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"8088ac","wall_time_seconds":0.3506131,"exit_code":1,"original_token_count":3446,"output":"\\r\\nFILE CONTEXT.md\\r\\n# Aspiring Attorneys tutoring operation\\r\\n\\r\\nThe language of Joshua\'s tutoring business as the brain models it: who a student\\r\\nis, how they pay, and how they get on the calendar. This is a glossary, not a\\r\\nspec. Implementation lives in the skills and scripts; decisions live in\\r\\n`decisions/log.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Language\\r\\n\\r\\n### Paying and scheduling\\r\\n\\r\\n**Package**:\\r\\nFive or ten hours of tutoring bought upfront through Stripe, with\\r\\nbetween-session coaching included.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Bundle, plan, subscription, credits (credits are the pass\'s units, not the product)\\r\\n\\r\\n**Booking pass**:\\r\\nThe prepaid entitlement a student holds when sessions are **owed** to them,\\r\\ncarrying the count owed and the count used. Reachable at `/book/<slug>`.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Booking link (that is the pass\'s URL, not the thing), booking access, pass token\\r\\n\\r\\n**Owed sessions**:\\r\\nSessions a student has paid for and not yet taken. The booking pass is the\\r\\n**only** record of these; if no pass exists, nothing is owed.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Balance, remaining sessions, package balance (there is no separate balance model)\\r\\n\\r\\n**Arrangement**:\\r\\nWhat a student actually bought, and when. A ten-hour package on a given date,\\r\\nor pay-per-session at a given rate. Authored on the student\'s page, durable,\\r\\nand never rewritten: it is history, not state.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Plan, deal, terms\\r\\n\\r\\n**Live status**:\\r\\nThe regenerated mirror of a student\'s current backend state, including how many\\r\\nowed sessions remain. Lives in `live-status.generated.md`, is rebuilt from the\\r\\nbackend, and is never hand-edited or hand-corrected.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Balance sheet, status page\\r\\n\\r\\n**Single session**:\\r\\nOne tutoring session paid for individually through its own Stripe-backed\\r\\nbooking link (the Cal.com link today; migrating to the platform\'s native\\r\\n`/book/session` page per lsatu ADR 0005), rather than out of a package.\\r\\nNeeds no booking pass.\\r\\n_Avoid_: One-off, ad hoc session, hourly\\r\\n\\r\\n**Three-tier offer**:\\r\\nThe standard offer presented after a consultation: a single session at $45\\r\\n(one 70-minute session), a five-hour package at $40 per hour, and a ten-hour\\r\\npackage at $35 per hour. Each offer carries its own verified action link. A prospect-specific\\r\\narrangement agreed during the consultation replaces the standard offer\\r\\nentirely.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Pricing menu, universal rate, package discount table\\r\\n\\r\\n**Consultation**:\\r\\nA free introductory call, booked through the Cal.com complimentary link. Does\\r\\nnot make the person a student.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Intro call, discovery call, free session\\r\\n\\r\\n**Consultation follow-up**:\\r\\nA message to a prospect who completed a consultation and has not paid. Payment\\r\\nends the consultation follow-up stage and moves the person into onboarding. The\\r\\nmessage presents the three tutoring offers so the prospect can choose how to\\r\\nbegin.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Onboarding email, post-payment email, student follow-up, application form email\\r\\n\\r\\n**Onboarding**:\\r\\nThe stage triggered by confirmed payment when a new student receives LSAT U\\r\\naccess and completes intake inside the platform. It does not require the person\\r\\nto reply to the consultation follow-up.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Consultation follow-up, application form, sales follow-up\\r\\n\\r\\n**LSAT U account creation**:\\r\\nCreating an account at `lsatprep.study`. A prospect may do this before payment,\\r\\nbut account creation alone does not grant student permissions or begin\\r\\nonboarding.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Onboarding, student access, enrollment\\r\\n\\r\\n### Relationship\\r\\n\\r\\nOne path, and everyone is on it. There is no separate category for a\\r\\nconsultation that never converted; that is a prospect who went quiet.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Prospect**:\\r\\nSomeone who has had a consultation but has never been taught a session.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Lead, inquiry, non-student\\r\\n\\r\\n**Active**:\\r\\nSomeone who is owed sessions, or who has been taught recently.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Current, enrolled, ongoing\\r\\n\\r\\n**Lapsed**:\\r\\nSomeone owed nothing who has gone quiet. Applies to a prospect who never\\r\\nconverted as much as to a student who stopped.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Churned, inactive, dormant, lost\\r\\n\\r\\n**Student record**:\\r\\nThe folder under `students/` holding everything authored about a person. A\\r\\nperson earns one by being **taught a session**, because teaching is what\\r\\nproduces notes, transcripts, and judgments the brain alone holds. A\\r\\nconsultation alone does not earn a record.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Profile, student file\\r\\n\\r\\n### Identity\\r\\n\\r\\n**Contact address**:\\r\\nThe address a student actually replies from, as evidenced in Gmail. The\\r\\nstudent page caches it; a booking pass merely records whatever was typed when\\r\\nthe pass was granted and is never authoritative for contact.\\r\\n_Avoid_: Email on file, registered email, account email\\r\\n\\r\\n**7Sage analytics URL**:\\r\\nThe shareable URL copied from a person\'s 7Sage Analytics page. Its canonical\\r\\nshape is `https://7sage.com/u/usr_<id>/review`. Stored in the `7sage_review:`\\r\\nfrontmatter key of `students/<name>/<name>.md`, which is its single source of\\r\\ntruth (the roster and profile mirror it).\\r\\n_Avoid_: sevensage (the platform is spelled **7Sage**), 7Sage login-token URL,\\r\\ndrill link, settings link, generic 7Sage URL\\r\\n\\r\\n**7Sage baseline**:\\r\\nThe one-time, full-history capture of a student\'s entire 7Sage analytics,\\r\\nwritten to `students/<name>/7sage/<date>-baseline.md` by the `7sage-onboarding`\\r\\nskill. The anchor every later pull measures against.\\r\\n_Avoid_: snapshot (a baseline is the complete history, not a moment), analytics dump\\r\\n\\r\\n**7Sage update (delta)**:\\r\\nA recurring pull of only what a student has done **since the last pull**, plus\\r\\nhow the tracked numbers moved, written to `<date>-update.md` by `7sage-update`.\\r\\nRequires a baseline to exist first.\\r\\n_Avoid_: re-scrape, refresh (both imply redoing the whole baseline)\\r\\n\\r\\n**7Sage profile**:\\r\\nThe single rolling current-state sheet at `students/<name>/7sage/profile.md`:\\r\\nwhere the student stands now and what to work on next, rewritten to a fixed\\r\\nschema on every pull. The one file session-prep reads.\\r\\n_Avoid_: summary, dashboard (it is a fixed-shape sheet, not a view)\\r\\n\\r\\n### Notes on the model\\r\\n\\r\\n- **A student with no booking pass is the normal case, not a gap.** It means\\r\\n  nothing is owed to them; they book single sessions through their Cal.com\\r\\n  link. Only a student who is owed sessions should hold a pass.\\r\\n- The two paths are not exclusive. Someone can hold a pass from a package and\\r\\n  also have a Cal.com single-session link.\\r\\n- A **revoked** pass is a dead link and must never be treated as a way to book.\\r\\n  An **exhausted** pass is live but has no owed sessions left.\\r\\n- Status is authored, not computed: it is a judgment about the relationship.\\r\\n  But hard evidence that contradicts it gets surfaced rather than silently\\r\\n  tolerated, the same way a contact address that disagrees with the backend\\r\\n  does.\\r\\n- A prospect who never converts has no record, but is still tracked by name so\\r\\n  follow-up remains possible. Losing sight of them is the failure this guards\\r\\n  against.\\r\\n- **Arrangement and live status are different things and live apart.** What was\\r\\n  bought is authored and permanent; what remains is mirrored and regenerated. A\\r\\n  count that is typed by hand is the failure mode, not the feature: the\\r\\n  hand-written mirrors drifted within a day of being written.\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\nFILE PLAN.md\\r\\n# Plan: Conditional Logic Cheat Sheet (LSAT U classroom note)\\r\\n_Round 0 � initial draft by Claude_\\r\\n\\r\\n## Goal\\r\\n\\r\\nA student-facing LSAT conditional-logic reference that renders inside the LSAT U\\r\\nclassroom as a `classroom_items` row (`content_html`, sanitized by DOMPurify\\r\\ndefault config, styled by `projects/lsatu/src/components/classroom/classroom.css`\\r\\nunder `.classroom-prose`). It must work as a *reference a student reaches for\\r\\nmid-drill*, not as a lesson. It is one of a pair; its sibling, The Breakdown\\r\\n(`corpus/concept-notes/conditionals-breakdown.lsatu.html`), teaches the same\\r\\nmaterial properly, one part at a time.\\r\\n\\r\\n**The artifact under review:** `corpus/concept-notes/conditionals-cheat-sheet.lsatu.html`\\r\\n\\r\\n## Current state (what is already built and live)\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **Grouped by what you do to the sentence**, using the class\'s own whiteboard\\r\\n   column names. The source class recording is\\r\\n   `lectures/conditional-logic/core/2026-05-28/script.md`; the instructor draws\\r\\n   four columns `LEFT / RIGHT / NEGATE LEFT / NEGATE RIGHT` (script.md:366 and\\r\\n   :475) and at 00:20:05 (script.md:556) says students who dislike the terms\\r\\n   *sufficient* and *necessary* \\"can also just say left and right.\\"\\r\\n   Section headings therefore read:\\r\\n   - 1. Words that put it on the left side of the arrow\\r\\n   - 2. Words that put it on the right side of the arrow\\r\\n   - 3. Words that negate the left side of the arrow\\r\\n   - 4. Words that negate the right side of the arrow\\r\\n   Sections 5-10 cover the traps: words on no list, and/or and the flip,\\r\\n   biconditionals, non-conditionals, the two illegal moves, when to draw at all.\\r\\n\\r\\n2. **Collapsible**, modelled on 7Sage\'s conditionals cheat sheet\\r\\n   (https://7sage.com/pages/free-lsat-resources-conditionals-cheat-sheet).\\r\\n   13 top-level `<details>`, all closed by default. Closed page height ~1550px,\\r\\n   down from ~9575px when it was flat. The list starts ~620px down the page so\\r\\n   roughly seven section names are visible without scrolling.\\r\\n\\r\\n3. **An example for every indicator word.** 44 words across the four groups\\r\\n   (19 / 16 / 4 / 5), each a table row: the word, a sentence using it, and the\\r\\n   arrow it produces. Two columns, arrow beneath the sentence, so nothing clips\\r\\n   at 375px.\\r\\n\\r\\n4. **A pattern panel per group**, taken from 7Sage\'s internal structure:\\r\\n   \\"It shows up like this\\" (sentence shapes using A and B), \\"Ask yourself\\"\\r\\n   (a diagnostic question that finds the trigger), \\"Which gives you\\"\\r\\n   (the bracket template rendered as tinted `left` / neutral `right` chips,\\r\\n   plus one worked example under a hairline).\\r\\n\\r\\n5. **Practice affordance:** each group has a \\"Diagram this\\" prompt with a bare\\r\\n   text input and a `<details>` \\"Check it\\" answer. Nothing is persisted.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Key decisions & tradeoffs (contest these)\\r\\n\\r\\n- **D1. Left/right vocabulary over sufficient/necessary.** The four group\\r\\n  headings name positions, not logical roles. *sufficient* and *necessary*\\r\\n  survive in exactly three places: the trigger/requirement diagram at the top,\\r\\n  the group-2 word list where *necessary* is itself an indicator, and the flaw\\r\\n  bridge (\\"when a flaw answer says the author confuses a necessary condition\\r\\n  with a sufficient one\\"). Tradeoff: the test\'s own answer choices use the\\r\\n  Latinate terms, so under-teaching them could cost points on Flaw questions.\\r\\n- **D2. One concrete example per word rather than 7Sage\'s head-word grouping.**\\r\\n  7Sage collapses variations under a head word (EVERY covers everyone,\\r\\n  everything, every time, everywhere). We keep all 44 words visible with an\\r\\n  example each. Tradeoff: longer, more repetitive, but nothing is hidden behind\\r\\n  a representative.\\r\\n- **D3. Everything collapsed by default, including \\"The four moves\\".** Nothing\\r\\n  is open on load. Tradeoff: a first-time reader sees only headings.\\r\\n- **D4. Inline styles only, driven by classroom CSS custom properties.** No\\r\\n  `<style>` block, because it would leak into the classroom globally. Tradeoff:\\r\\n  no media queries, so every layout must be width-agnostic. This already forced\\r\\n  one rebuild when a three-column table clipped at 375px.\\r\\n- **D5. Group 3 is named \\"negate the left\\" though its rule sends the attached\\r\\n  idea right.** This matches the class board\'s `NEGATE LEFT` column, which is\\r\\n  about *unless* meaning \\"whatever you put on the left, negate it.\\" Tradeoff:\\r\\n  the heading and the mechanic can read as contradictory on first pass.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Risks / open questions\\r\\n\\r\\n- **R1. Wording.** Joshua\'s standing note is that drafts do not sound like his\\r\\n  teaching. Which sentences still read as written-by-a-machine rather than said\\r\\n  in class? Be specific and quote them.\\r\\n- **R2. Layout.** Is the per-group pattern panel plus a 19-row table too much\\r\\n  inside one accordion section? Is there a better division?\\r\\n- **R3. Are any of the 44 examples wrong, ambiguous, or badly chosen?**\\r\\n  Especially group 2 bare *only* (\\"Only members may vote\\"), group 1\\r\\n  *provided that*, and group 3 *except*.\\r\\n- **R4. Redundancy.** \\"The four moves\\" section, the per-group rule paragraph,\\r\\n  and \\"All ten, in four steps\\" arguably say the same thing three times.\\r\\n- **R5. Does anything contradict the source class?** The class recording is the\\r\\n  authority. `corpus/concept-notes/conditionals-breakdown.lsatu.html` is the\\r\\n  sibling and must not disagree with this file.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Voice rules (hard constraints, non-negotiable)\\r\\n\\r\\n- No em dashes anywhere.\\r\\n- No AI-slop vocabulary (delve, tapestry, testament, demystify, boasts,\\r\\n  \\"in the realm of\\").\\r\\n- No quantifying what an action costs (\\"costs you time\\", \\"worth the two\\r\\n  seconds\\"). MAX_ROUNDS=5. Model gpt-5.6-sol, reasoning effort forced high.\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\n## Round 1 — Codex (gpt-5.6-sol, reasoning high)\\r\\n\\r\\nThis needs revision. The artifact contains two outright logic errors, several unreliable indicator examples, and a layout that behaves like a compressed lesson rather than a mid-drill reference.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Source and logic conflicts\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **Indicator words are overstated.** “One word tells you which is which” conflicts with the transcript, which says some conditionals have no indicator and must be recognized from universality, requirements, or certainty. When there is no indicator, ask what triggers the rule and what must follow.\\r\\n\\r\\n2. **“Read every rule as the left requires the right” is too absolute.** The instructor says students may read a rule as either “if left, then right” or “left requires right,” depending on which sounds natural. Rewrite:\\r\\n\\r\\n   > Replace “unless B” with “if not B.” Put NOT B on the left and the other idea on the right.\\r\\n\\r\\n5. **The `no` section hides the symmetry taught in class.** The instructor taught that either term may go left, provided the other term is negated on the right. Rewrite:\\r\\n\\r\\n   > With “no,” either idea can go on the left. Put the other idea on the right and negate it. The instructor also explicitly lists `depends` and `prerequisite` as requirement language. Delete this section and retain only the fallback question about trigger and requirement.\\r\\n\\r\\n7. **`Otherwise` is taught incorrectly.** From `NOT deposit → released`, the contrapositive `NOT released → deposit` is the same rule, not a second direction. It does not prove “Either both things happen or neither does.” Delete `otherwise` from the biconditional section. The transcript instead teaches:\\r\\n\\r\\n   - if and only if\\r\\n   - all that is required\\r\\n   - all that is needed\\r\\n\\r\\n8. **The artifact avoids the word `contrapositive`.** The instructor repeatedly names contraposition as a core skill. Every summary should say:\\r\\n\\r\\n   > Swap the sides, negate every term, and switch AND ↔ OR.\\r\\n\\r\\n10. **The necessary/sufficient flaw is narrowed incorrectly.** The HTML says it means claiming the left from the right. Do not draw” is too categorical. Rewrite:\\r\\n\\r\\n   > If you are still learning, write the rule and its contrapositive. The remaining rows diagram correctly as written.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Indicator | Problem | Replacement |\\r\\n|---|---|---|\\r\\n| `wherever` | “Wherever the trail floods, it closes” has unclear spatial scope and an ambiguous `it`. | “Wherever the river floods the road, that stretch closes.” `flooded stretch → closed stretch` |\\r\\n| `provided that` | Permission language makes waiting sound necessary, while the table wants the formal sufficient reading. | “Provided that the badge scans, the door opens.” `badge scans → door opens` |\\r\\n| `leads to` | “Skipping review leads to repeat errors” states a tendency, not a guarantee. | “Under the policy, submitting late leads to automatic rejection.” `late → rejected` |\\r\\n| `produces` | “Heat produces expansion” is not universally true without qualifications. | “In this machine, pressing START produces a receipt.” `press START → receipt` |\\r\\n| `guarantees` | A 175 does not generally guarantee an interview. it does not create a conditional. The generic plural is doing the universal work. | Replace with `nobody`: “Nobody under 18 entered.” |\\r\\n\\r\\nAlso rewrite “Whenever she drills, she reviews it after” as:\\r\\n\\r\\n> Whenever she finishes a drill, she reviews every question she missed.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Machine-written wording\\r\\n\\r\\nThese are the clearest offenders.\\r\\n\\r\\n- “Every conditional is one sentence with a trigger and a requirement.”\\r\\n\\r\\n  > A conditional has two parts: a trigger and what that trigger requires.\\r\\n\\r\\n- “One word tells you which is which. Anything behind a login goes to **agent Chrome on 9222** (`references/agent-browser.md`, profile `~/.agent-chrome`, driven by the `chrome-devtools` MCP, relaunched at every login by `com.aspiringattorneys.agent-chrome`) - Firecrawl\'s browser runs in Firecrawl\'s cloud and does not have his cookies. **Obsidian\'s Web viewer on 9223** (`references/obsidian-browser.md`) is for reading inside the vault. **Playwright is the fallback of last resort: only when Firecrawl has actually been tried and failed, and only after asking him first and getting a yes.** Never reach for it silently, and never for a page needing a login - its throwaway profile lands logged out and forces him to sign in again. Same for the built-in Claude Code browser. Cookies live in the profile and persist across reboots, so a site he signs into once stays signed in indefinitely. Reading in a signed-in session is standing-authorized; send/post/publish/delete still need his yes. Agents never type credentials - he signs in once per site, with Proton Pass filling it, and that is the last time |\\r\\n| How-it-works reference notes (methods, not state) | `references/` — also `references/claude-plus-codex.md`, `references/obsidian-command-center.md`, `references/second-brain-levels.md` |\\r\\n| Immutable source evidence · intake queue | `raw/sources/` · `raw/inbox/` |\\r\\n| **Graph homes — three, and none is redundant** | `graph/` = graphify v8 subgraph output (`brain/`, `lsatu/`, `obsidian-view/`). `graph-imports/` = **build inputs**, four of them load-bearing: `build-unified-graph.py` declares `vault-run/`, `vault-run-b2/`, `vault-run-b3/`, `graph-imports/graphify-out/` as mandatory `FRAGMENTS`; `vault/`, `vault-b2/`, `vault-b3/` are generated concept-note vaults the log still cites. `graphify-out/` = the unified whole-brain graph, at graphify\'s own conventional output path (also hard-coded in `build-freshness.py`). **Do not \\"tidy\\" these into one folder** — a 2026-08-12 cleanup plan proposed exactly that and would have broken the unified graph build. Follow links; there is no semantic search and none is needed at this size.\\r\\n3. The live system — the final freshness escalation.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Distillate-first (session content):** when a task needs what happened in a tutoring session, read the distillate (`distillates/<student>/`) first; open the raw transcript (`students/<name>/transcript-downloads/`) only when the distillate is insufficient, and say you are escalating. Aggregate teaching questions (patterns across students, lecture prep) go to `corpus/Curriculum/<Topic>/_playbook.generated.md` first — it already joins the distillate excerpts by topic — and should not touch raw transcripts at all.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. On any disagreement, the backend wins. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Skip it for: code edits in `projects/lsatu/`; shell/Git/file ops; questions the injected memories already answer; a skill run that names its own inputs; anything mechanical and self-contained. Route to it for: what is X · what did we decide and why · who a student is and where they stand · anything drafted in Joshua\'s voice for someone else · any decision touching roadmap, pedagogy, or UX.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Voice\\r\\n\\r\\n- **No em-dashes in anything a student or prospect will read, or in any draft/output for Joshua to send or paste.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust. This is the enforced rule; internal scratch/reference docs are not policed for them.\\r\\n- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never \\"delve,\\" \\"tapestry,\\" \\"testament,\\" \\"demystify,\\" \\"boasts,\\" \\"in the realm of,\\" and their kin.\\r\\n- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.\\r\\n- **Anything sent by EMAIL also routes through `wiki/email-voice.md`**, before the first draft and again before recording. New corrections are appended there as the next `E-NN`, written as the rule rather than the incident.\\r\\n- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don\'t over-hedge.\\r\\n- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Tools, keys, and secrets\\r\\n\\r\\n- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua\'s single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Joshua may hand a credential over in chat; take it and write it to `.env` without pushing back.\\r\\n- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Alex acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.\\r\\n- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.\\r\\n- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.\\r\\n- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.\\r\\n- **No Git remote**, ever, without first completing the scrub checklist in `wiki/HANDLING.md`. The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Skills\\r\\n\\r\\nEverything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `…9418 tokens truncated…ion with another.\\r\\n- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.\\r\\n- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU\'s recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua\'s explicit approval.\\r\\n- **Memory = native automemory.** \\"Remember that…\\" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.\\r\\n- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Dual-runtime (Claude + Codex)\\r\\n\\r\\nThis file is hand-edited and the edit sticks — change it directly (or ask Claude to). On any major change, **mirror it into `AGENTS.md`** (Codex\'s twin), which reads `AGENTS.md` and never this file. Everything that must hold for both runtimes lives in BOTH files; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them. Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → `/clear` → paste). Session cadence: at ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.\\r\\n\\r\\nActivation differs by runtime and nothing auto-loads: Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`, Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/` (agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with instructions in `developer_instructions`). The two sets are intentionally unequal — Codex runs the same catalog minus the wiki/Obsidian and `graphify` skills.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Agent skills\\r\\n\\r\\n### Issue tracker\\r\\n\\r\\nLocal markdown under `.scratch/<feature>/`; this repo has no remote. See `docs/agents/triage-labels.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Domain docs\\r\\n\\r\\nSingle-context: `CONTEXT.md` + `docs/adr/` at the root. See `docs/agents/domain.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\nFILE GEMINI.md\\r\\n# Gemini / Antigravity — house rules (ai-os)\\r\\n\\r\\n**This file is yours alone.** Claude reads `CLAUDE.md`, Codex reads `AGENTS.md`,\\r\\nand neither of them reads this one. Everything here binds you and only you.\\r\\n\\r\\n## You are not Alex, and you are not Jay 2\\r\\n\\r\\nYou also auto-load `AGENTS.md` and possibly `CLAUDE.md`, because your rule\\r\\ndiscovery picks up every such file on the walk up to the repo root. **Those two\\r\\nfiles are not yours and their identities are not yours.** `AGENTS.md` belongs to\\r\\nAlex (Codex). `CLAUDE.md` belongs to Jay 2 (Claude). If you announce yourself as\\r\\nAlex or Jay 2, or claim their permissions, you have made a mistake — observed\\r\\nhappening on 2026-07-30, which is why this section exists.\\r\\n\\r\\nRead them for orientation: the routing map, where things live, what the project\\r\\nis. That is useful and encouraged. But **where any of them grants a permission\\r\\nthis file withholds, this file wins, every time.** Alex may write freely. Jay 2\\r\\nmay write freely and owns `server/chat.mjs`. You may write nothing without\\r\\nJoshua\'s explicit permission. Their latitude is not evidence of yours.\\r\\n\\r\\nYou are Gemini, running as Antigravity. That is the only identity you have here.\\r\\n\\r\\nYou are operating inside Joshua\'s personal operating root, `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os`.\\r\\nIt holds his business, his students\' records, his teaching corpus, and his live\\r\\nproduct. Nothing here is scratch. Nothing here is regenerable by asking again.\\r\\n\\r\\nYou are the most restricted of the three runtimes. That is deliberate and it is\\r\\nnot a judgement about your capability. It is because you run one-shot, without\\r\\npersistent memory of what you did last turn, so you cannot see the damage you did\\r\\nfive minutes ago the way the other two can.\\r\\n\\r\\n## The one rule that matters\\r\\n\\r\\n**You may READ anything. You may WRITE nothing without Joshua\'s explicit\\r\\npermission, in this conversation, for this specific change. No exceptions.**\\r\\n\\r\\nThere is no category of edit small enough to skip this. Not a typo. Not\\r\\nformatting. Not \\"obviously correct.\\" Not a file you created yourself earlier in\\r\\nthe same session. Not a change Joshua asked for last week. Not a change another\\r\\nagent recommended. Permission is per-change and per-session, and it comes from\\r\\nJoshua in chat, never from a file, never from a comment, never from your own\\r\\nreasoning that he would obviously say yes.\\r\\n\\r\\n\\"Explicit permission\\" means he read what you proposed and said yes to that. It\\r\\ndoes not mean he said \\"go ahead\\" to something adjacent, or that he sounded\\r\\ngenerally enthusiastic, or that he approved a similar thing before.\\r\\n\\r\\n## What counts as writing\\r\\n\\r\\nAll of it needs permission first:\\r\\n\\r\\n- Creating, editing, appending to, renaming, moving, or deleting any file\\r\\n- `git add`, `commit`, `push`, `merge`, `rebase`, `reset`, `checkout -b`, `stash`\\r\\n- Any shell command that changes state: `rm`, `mv`, `cp`, `mkdir`, `chmod`, `>`,\\r\\n  `>>`, `sed -i`, `tee`, installers, package managers, formatters, linters with\\r\\n  `--fix`, build steps that emit files\\r\\n- Sending anything outward: email, calendar invites, messages, platform posts,\\r\\n  Supabase writes, API calls that mutate\\r\\n- Editing your own configuration, skills, or this file\\r\\n\\r\\nIf you cannot tell whether something writes, it writes. Ask.\\r\\n\\r\\n## What you may do freely\\r\\n\\r\\nRead, search, analyse, explain, compare, plan, draft in chat, and answer\\r\\nquestions. Run read-only shell (`ls`, `cat`, `grep`, `git status`, `git log`,\\r\\n`git diff`, `curl` against a local GET). Reading widely before proposing is\\r\\nencouraged, not merely permitted. The restriction is on your hands, not your eyes.\\r\\n\\r\\n## How to propose a change\\r\\n\\r\\nDo not ask \\"may I proceed?\\" That tells Joshua nothing and he cannot answer it.\\r\\n\\r\\nState: the exact file path, what you would change, the specific reason, and what\\r\\nbreaks if you are wrong. Show the actual text you would write when it is short.\\r\\nThen stop and wait. One proposal at a time.\\r\\n\\r\\nIf he says no, that is the end of it. Do not re-argue it, do not find a smaller\\r\\nversion of it, and do not do it anyway in a different file.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Files you must never write, even with permission asked casually\\r\\n\\r\\n- `server/chat.mjs` in `projects/command-center/` — **Claude-write-only** by\\r\\n  Joshua\'s decision of 2026-07-30. It is the single chokepoint every chat panel\\r\\n  depends on and an edit here was already lost once to a concurrent rewrite.\\r\\n  Read it freely; you need it to understand how the server spawns and talks to\\r\\n  you. If it needs changing, say so in chat and let Claude land it.\\r\\n- `CLAUDE.md` and `AGENTS.md` anywhere in the vault — those belong to the other\\r\\n  two runtimes.\\r\\n- `.env` anywhere. You may read it. You may APPEND to it ONLY when very explicitly requested by Joshua. You are NEVER allowed to delete or overwrite anything from it at any point, ever. Do not paste a credential into chat under any framing.\\r\\n- Anything under `raw/` — immutable source evidence. Read only, always.\\r\\n- `projects/lsatu/` — its own repository with its own instructions, and its\\r\\n  recovered `CLAUDE.md` / `AGENTS.md` are inviolate.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Standing guardrails\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Never delete or move a file or folder.** This one does not have a permission\\r\\n  path in practice. Ask, expect to be told no, and propose an alternative.\\r\\n- **No git remote push, ever**, without the scrub checklist in `wiki/HANDLING.md`\\r\\n  completed first. The vault contains student PII.\\r\\n- **Never fake data.** No invented numbers, no placeholder metrics presented as\\r\\n  real, no progress or analytics that does not trace to something stored. If a\\r\\n  value is unavailable, say so in words.\\r\\n- **Verify by real runs, not by reading code.** If you claim something works,\\r\\n  you ran it. If you did not run it, say you did not.\\r\\n- **Never impersonate Joshua.** Anything a student or prospect would read is a\\r\\n  draft for his review, never a send, and it follows `wiki/teaching-voice.md`.\\r\\n- **One command per shell call.** Chained forms trip permission denials the\\r\\n  split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep` in the foreground.\\r\\n- **Report honestly.** If a step failed, was skipped, or you are unsure, say that\\r\\n  plainly in your summary. A confident wrong answer costs more than a hedge.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Transcript intake: three files are append-only, no exceptions\\r\\n\\r\\nWhen filing a session transcript (transcript-downloads, distillate,\\r\\nteaching-notes.md, open-loops.md, transcripts.md), three of those files —\\r\\nteaching-notes.md, open-loops.md, transcripts.md — are APPEND-ONLY. On\\r\\n2026-08-04 you got this right for Adriana Varea\'s teaching-notes.md and\\r\\ntranscripts.md: you added a new dated section or row at the end and touched\\r\\nnothing else. You got it wrong for her open-loops.md: you deleted two whole\\r\\nsections (\\"Unclosed from the last session (2026-06-02)\\" and \\"The actual\\r\\ndecision\\") and one open item instead of leaving them alone or closing them\\r\\nproperly. That was real information loss — a live commitment, an unresolved\\r\\nfollow-up, and a durable judgment call about re-contact strategy, gone\\r\\nwithout a trace, in a vault with no other copy. It had to be reconstructed\\r\\nfrom git history and re-applied by hand.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe rule, precisely: never delete or rewrite an existing line or section in\\r\\nthese three files. To add new information, add it at the end, or in a new\\r\\ndated subsection. To mark something resolved, strike it with ~~...~~ and\\r\\nappend \\"Closed <date>: <what happened>\\" — the old text stays, readable,\\r\\nforever. If you cannot tell from the transcript whether something was\\r\\nresolved, leave it open exactly as it was — do not guess, and do not drop it\\r\\nbecause a new session happened nearby. The corrected version of\\r\\nstudents/adriana-varea/open-loops.md (fixed 2026-08-04) is the reference\\r\\nexample: old items struck through with a Closed note, new items appended,\\r\\nnothing removed.\\r\\n\\r\\nBefore you touch any of these three files going forward, read this section\\r\\nfirst.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Where to find things\\r\\n\\r\\nThe routing map lives in `CLAUDE.md` at this same root. You may read it for\\r\\norientation. Read it as a map, not as your instructions: where the two files\\r\\ndisagree about what you are permitted to do, this file wins.\\r\\n\\r\\n## If you are unsure\\r\\n\\r\\nStop and ask. An unnecessary question costs Joshua ten seconds. An unrequested\\r\\nwrite can cost work that has no backup and no upstream copy, in a vault whose\\r\\nprimary records exist nowhere else.\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\nFILE connections.md\\r\\n# Connections\\r\\n\\r\\nRegistry of every live system Jay 2 can reach, mapped to the 7 Tier-1 data domains. The `/audit` skill reads this for domain coverage and freshness. When a new tool is wired, add a row **and** (if it has an API) save `references/{tool}-api.md` — researched-once-saved-forever.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Access rule:** all Google surfaces go through the `gws` CLI (never the connector MCPs). All secrets live in the root `.env`. See CLAUDE.md → \\"Tools, keys, and connections.\\"\\r\\n\\r\\n| # | Domain | Tool | Mechanism | Auth | Last checked |\\r\\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|\\r\\n| 1 | Revenue / Financials | Stripe | `STRIPE_API_KEY` (`.env`, `rk_live_` restricted) — REST; read verified, payment-link/price writes need scope grant | active | 2026-07-24 |\\r\\n| 2 | Customer interactions | Gmail-as-CRM · TutorOcean | `gws` CLI · manual | active | 2026-07-17 |\\r\\n| 3 | Calendar | Cal.com · Google Calendar | Cal.com: `CALCOM_API_KEY` (`.env`, `cal_live_`) — **API v2 only**; Google Cal: `gws` CLI | active | 2026-07-24 |\\r\\n| 4 | Communication | Gmail | `gws` CLI | active | 2026-07-17 |\\r\\n| 5 | Project / task tracking | `wiki/tasks.md` ledger | local file (no external tracker) | n/a | 2026-07-17 |\\r\\n| 6 | Meeting intelligence | Gmail/Drive session notes · Gemini Meet notes | `gws` CLI (export→`raw/`) | active | 2026-07-17 |\\r\\n| 7 | Knowledge / files | Google Drive · this vault | `gws` CLI · local | active | 2026-07-17 |\\r\\n| 7b | LSAT U backend | Lovable Cloud `atrcqxpmqrynmtvvyjju` | **Lovable MCP** (`claude.ai Lovable`): `query_database` full SQL+DDL (free), `send_message` code+edge-fn deploy (credits); also admin-JWT REST (`LSATPREP_ADMIN_*`) | active | 2026-07-24 |\\r\\n| 8 | Code / VCS | GitHub | local MCP server (`@modelcontextprotocol/server-github`) | `GITHUB_PERSONAL_ACCESS_TOKEN` (`.env`) | active | 2026-07-25 |\\r\\n\\r\\n**Web / browser access (primary path, set 2026-07-24):** for anything on the web or in a logged-in app, use **claude-in-chrome** — Joshua\'s real, already-logged-in Chrome (extension enabled, \\"Allow all sites\\"). Navigate the extension\'s controlled tab to the target URL; Chrome uses his \\"Work\\" profile, so it lands already signed in. For the open web (search, scrape, research) use **Firecrawl** (per CLAUDE.md\'s Firecrawl-first rule). **Do not use Playwright or chrome-devtools-mcp** unless Joshua explicitly asks; the built-in Claude Code browser is only a fallback when the extension is disconnected. Details in memory [[browser-backend-default]].\\r\\n\\r\\n**Tier-2 (bonus):** NotebookLM (`nlm` CLI), YouTube Data API (`.env`), Superwhisper (audio/video → text; `system/scripts/transcribe.sh`).\\r\\n\\r\\n**Mechanism legend:** `mcp` (MCP server) · `script` (Python/Bash hitting an API) · `export` (dump pipeline) · `key+ref` (`.env` key + `references/{tool}-api.md`) · `local` (file in this vault) · `not yet connected`.\\r\\n\\r\\n**The LSAT U backend: what Jay 2 can and cannot do.** Verified end-to-end 2026-07-20.\\r\\n\\r\\n\\"Lovable\\" and \\"Supabase\\" are not two backends. **Lovable Cloud IS a managed Supabase stack**\\r\\n(Postgres, Auth, Storage, Edge Functions) that Lovable provisions and operates. That is why\\r\\nboth names appear. Full platform detail in [[wiki/lovable-cloud]]; live schema in\\r\\n[[wiki/lsatu-backend-map]].\\r\\n\\r\\n**CAN do programmatically** — no browser, no paste. Authenticate as admin against the public\\r\\nauth API with `LSATPREP_ADMIN_EMAIL` / `LSATPREP_ADMIN_PASSWORD` from the root `.env`, using\\r\\nthe anon key in `projects/lsatu/.env`, then call PostgREST and the deployed edge functions\\r\\nwith that JWT. Verified: token issued, and `booking_passes` read back through RLS as admin.\\r\\nThis covers essentially all **data**-level work — reading and writing rows, calling RPCs like\\r\\n`grant_booking_access` and `regenerate_booking_link`, driving the booking/homework/messaging\\r\\nadmin functions. The JWT expires in 3600s; re-auth rather than caching it.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Since 2026-07-24 — the Lovable MCP connector supersedes most of the old limits.** The\\r\\n`claude.ai Lovable` connector (see [[skills/claude/lovable-mcp]]) is wired and scopes to\\r\\nJoshua\'s whole Lovable account. Through it: `query_database` runs **full SQL including DDL and\\r\\nmigrations** directly (free, no agent, no admin JWT) — so schema changes are no longer\\r\\nLovable-UI-only; `send_message` edits **and deploys edge functions and frontend code** (this\\r\\nis the only credit-spending path); `read_file` / `get_diff` / `list_files` / `deploy_project`\\r\\n/ analytics are free. The `lovable-migrate` UI path is now a fallback, not the only route.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Still genuinely out of reach:** edge-function **runtime logs** (no access at all — debug by\\r\\ninspecting DB state via `query_database` and reading the deployed source via `read_file`).\\r\\nThere is still no service-role key or raw DB connection string outside Lovable, by design;\\r\\nsecrets, storage file browsing/download, backups/restore, and instance sizing remain\\r\\nLovable-console operations. Note pushed local migrations do **not** auto-apply — apply schema\\r\\nchanges through `query_database` (or the Lovable UI), don\'t assume a `git push` ran them.\\r\\n\\r\\n**HAZARD — never resolve the project by name.** The connected Supabase MCP sees only\\r\\nJoshua\'s *own* org `ostsvacinxuehptsniry`, which does not contain the live backend but does\\r\\ncontain a different project *also named \\"LSATU\\"* (`tlqltiklhmrceksqnwsl`, ACTIVE_HEALTHY).\\r\\nSQL aimed at \\"the LSATU project\\" via the MCP applies cleanly to the wrong database and\\r\\nreports success. Match the ref `atrcqxpmqrynmtvvyjju` exactly, or do not run it. The\\r\\n**Supabase** MCP is not a path to this backend at all — use the **Lovable** MCP\\r\\n(`query_database`, which targets the live Cloud DB directly) instead.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Superwhisper — the transcription engine (Domains 6 + 7).** Verified end-to-end 2026-07-22.\\r\\n\\r\\nLocal macOS app (Pro) that turns any audio/video file into text. It is the default\\r\\ntranscription path for the `transcribe`, `ingest`, and `transcript-intake` skills.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Mechanism:** `open <file> -a superwhisper` queues the file for transcription using the\\r\\n  currently active mode. Formats: MP3, MP4, M4A, WAV, WEBM (video included). Supports\\r\\n  speaker separation (`separateSpeakersEnabled`). Wrapper: `system/scripts/transcribe.sh\\r\\n  <file>` runs it and prints the output `meta.json` path.\\r\\n- **Output:** each job writes `~/superwhisper/recordings/<epoch-id>/meta.json` — read\\r\\n  `.result` (clean transcript), `.segments`, `.speakers`, `.duration`, `.datetime`. Nothing\\r\\n  to parse; it is JSON. The source file is never modified.\\r\\n- **Auth:** none for the app itself. **Cloud caveat:** the active \\"Default\\" mode currently\\r\\n  uses **S1-Voice (Cloud)**, so audio leaves the machine. A local Whisper model\\r\\n  (`ggml-large-v3-turbo.bin`) is already on disk. **For student-PII recordings, switch the\\r\\n  mode to the local model in the Superwhisper app before transcribing.** Joshua\'s own\\r\\n  teaching videos over cloud are fine.\\r\\n- **Separate feature (not transcription):** the `superwhisper@superwhisper` Claude Code\\r\\n  plugin (enabled in `~/.claude/settings.json`) is a voice-reply loop — it surfaces the app\\r\\n  on Stop / Notification / AskUserQuestion / PermissionRequest so Joshua can answer Claude\\r\\n  Code by voice. Leave it on; it does not touch the transcription pipeline.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Cal.com API — booking/scheduling (Domain 3).** Verified end-to-end 2026-07-24.\\r\\n\\r\\nPersonal API key `CALCOM_API_KEY` in the root `.env` (`cal_live_…`), acts as Joshua\\r\\n(Cal user `1556305`, `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`, handle `joshua-jtx5ud`). Full\\r\\nread+write on his own account.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **API v2 ONLY.** v1 is decommissioned (`api.cal.com/v1/*` returns a migration notice).\\r\\n  Call `https://api.cal.com/v2/…` with `Authorization: [REDACTED] $CALCOM_API_KEY` **and** a\\r\\n  `cal-api-version` header (e.g. `2024-06-14` for event-types, `2024-08-13` for `/me`).\\r\\n- **Verified calls:** `GET /v2/me` (identity); `GET /v2/event-types?username=joshua-jtx5ud`\\r\\n  → 12 event types. Booking-link pattern is `cal.com/joshua-jtx5ud/<slug>`. Live visible\\r\\n  slugs: `consultation` (30m), `lsat` (65m); key hidden helpers: `session-link` (70m LSAT\\r\\n  Session), `complimentary-session` (45m), `c-reduced-rate` (70m), `reduced-rate`,\\r\\n  `tutoring` (Dakota), `canadian-scheduling`. Canonical send-list in [[context/links]].\\r\\n- **Capabilities:** list/create/edit event types, bookings, availability as Joshua. Per the\\r\\n  operating principle, link/event-type generation runs freely; anything that charges a\\r\\n  customer or mutates a live booking/calendar event is confirmed with Joshua first.\\r\\n- Save the endpoint reference to `references/calcom-api.md` when the generator is built.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Notes / gaps:**\\r\\n- Task tracking is deliberately the `wiki/tasks.md` Now/Next/Done ledger, not an external tool (ClickUp/Asana). This is the intended design — the wiki is the tracker.\\r\\n- Session-note ingestion (Domain 6) is the highest-leverage under-used pipe: ~191 notes/yr, <10% currently ingested. Target of the planned post-session dispatch skill.\\r\\n\\r\\n"}'}]

Generated by `system/scripts/build-rc-passage-index.py` |\\r\\n| **A lecture to teach** (scripts he can stand up and deliver) | `lectures/README.md` — router. `<series>/core\\\\|advanced/<date>/script.md` is the deliverable: one **beat** per screen state, every board as an image at the moment it belongs to, words verbatim, questions attached. Source of truth is each `students/<slug>/open-loops.md`; close a task there and rebuild with `system/scripts/build-task-board.py`. Written after every session by the `session-tasks` skill, whose line format is `.claude/skills/session-tasks/references/task-format.md` |\\r\\n| **What counts as a task** (and how that judgment improves) | `wiki/task-rules.md` — append-only `T-NN` ledger, sibling of `wiki/email-voice.md`. `session-tasks` reads it before every extraction and appends the next `T-NN` whenever Joshua corrects one. Sessions are never reprocessed: the `## Action items from <date> session` heading in `open-loops.md` is the receipt, written even for a session with no tasks |\\r\\n| What a session taught (read BEFORE the raw transcript) | `distillates/<student>/` — rule at `distillates/README.md` |\\r\\n| Is anything in the brain stale? | `wiki/freshness.generated.md` — regenerate via `system/scripts/build-freshness.py` |\\r\\n| Voice for anything a student or prospect reads | `wiki/teaching-voice.md` |\\r\\n| **Voice for an EMAIL specifically** (the running rule set) | `wiki/email-voice.md` — append-only ledger of every correction Joshua has made to a drafted email and the rule it implies. Read before drafting any email, checked against before recording. Its sibling for teaching notes is `Cowork/post-session-notes/line-edits.md`; the two are separate because a session recap and a note to a prospect are different registers. A correction that lands only in chat is one the button makes again next week |\\r\\n| Every link Joshua sends (never send one not here) | `context/links.md` — generated; declare in `context/links.json`, look up with `link <purpose>` |\\r\\n| **A GIF to attach to an email or doc** | `gifs/` — the attachable GIF library: `gifs.json` declares each one (what it shows, what to attach it to, who it is for, whether it is email-safe), `README.md` is the readable view, `CLAUDE.md` the rules. Never attach a GIF that is not declared there, and never send an onboarding GIF to a prospect who has not paid |\\r\\n| Golden templates + the four triggers | `context/templates.md` — generated; declare in `context/templates.json`, look up with `template <id>` |\\r\\n| Which skill owns a reply (situation × source) | `context/drafting.md` — generated; declare in `context/drafting.json`, look up with `draft-for --situation <id> --source <src>`. Exit code is the branch: 0 skill · 1 free-draft · 2 chat-only · 3 refuse · 4 unknown |\\r\\n| How any email reaches the green check mark | `context/draft-contract.md` — one `vault-draft record` call; a draft not on the board does not exist |\\r\\n| Was it sent? (every outbound draft/send) | `wiki/send-ledger.md` — append-only; Gmail is authority |\\r\\n| **When Joshua last saw a person** (student or prospect) | `wiki/last-seen.md` — one row per person, Last seen / Prior; calendar is authority, this is the fast cache. Rolled forward by the post-meeting intake tasks |\\r\\n| **The daily agent schedule** (who runs what, when, from the calendar) | `references/morning-dispatch.md` — rule set the recurring `morning-dispatch` scheduled task (7:30 AM, `~/.claude/scheduled-tasks/`) executes: per-consultation intakes, pre-session 7Sage prep, post-session intake + task extraction, evening follow-up drafts |\\r\\n| Who the students are (name, email, sessions, links) | `students/roster.generated.md` — regenerate, never hand-edit |\\r\\n| Learned facts and preferences (automemory) | native store `~/.claude/projects/-Users-joshuaf-ai-os/memory/` + `MEMORY.md` index |\\r\\n| Skill / agent catalog | `skills/INDEX.md`, `agents/` |\\r\\n| Subagent teams — Cleanup Crew charter + roster | `agents/SUBAGENTS.md` — active copies in `.claude/agents/`; delegate in-domain work to the crew |\\r\\n| Systems Jay 2 can reach (registry) | `connections.md` — 7 data domains, mechanism/auth/freshness; read by `/audit` |\\r\\n| Operator brain (how to think about automation) | `references/3ms-framework.md` — the Three Ms; walked weekly by `/level-up` |\\r\\n| Automation build backlog (what to build next, ranked) | `references/automation-backlog.md` — corpus-derived gaps + tiers; feeds `/level-up` |\\r\\n| **A browser that is always up, already signed in** | **Firecrawl is the base; agent Chrome owns anything with a login; Playwright only with his say-so.** Public web - read, search, or interact (clicks, forms, pagination, infinite scroll) - goes to **Firecrawl** first, every time (`firecrawl scrape|search|interact`). Anything behind a login goes to **agent Chrome on 9222** (`references/agent-browser.md`, profile `~/.agent-chrome`, driven by the `chrome-devtools` MCP, relaunched at every login by `com.aspiringattorneys.agent-chrome`) - Firecrawl\'s browser runs in Firecrawl\'s cloud and does not have his cookies. **Obsidian\'s Web viewer on 9223** (`references/obsidian-browser.md`) is for reading inside the vault. **Playwright is the fallback of last resort: only when Firecrawl has actually been tried and failed, and only after asking him first and getting a yes.** Never reach for it silently, and never for a page needing a login - its throwaway profile lands logged out and forces him to sign in again. Same for the built-in Claude Code browser. Cookies live in the profile and persist across reboots, so a site he signs into once stays signed in indefinitely. Reading in a signed-in session is standing-authorized; send/post/publish/delete still need his yes. Agents never type credentials - he signs in once per site, with Proton Pass filling it, and that is the last time |\\r\\n| How-it-works reference notes (methods, not state) | `references/` — also `references/claude-plus-codex.md`, `references/obsidian-command-center.md`, `references/second-brain-levels.md` |\\r\\n| Immutable source evidence · intake queue | `raw/sources/` · `raw/inbox/` |\\r\\n| **Graph homes — three, and none is redundant** | `graph/` = graphify v8 subgraph output (`brain/`, `lsatu/`, `obsidian-view/`). `graph-imports/` = **build inputs**, four of them load-bearing: `build-unified-graph.py` declares `vault-run/`, `vault-run-b2/`, `vault-run-b3/`, `graph-imports/graphify-out/` as mandatory `FRAGMENTS`; `vault/`, `vault-b2/`, `vault-b3/` are generated concept-note vaults the log still cites. `graphify-out/` = the unified whole-brain graph, at graphify\'s own conventional output path (also hard-coded in `build-freshness.py`). **Do not \\"tidy\\" these into one folder** — a 2026-08-12 cleanup plan proposed exactly that and would have broken the unified graph build. All three are gitignored, PII-derived, local-only. |\\r\\n| Prospect material — three homes, one authority | `students/pipeline/` is **authoritative** (the state machine: `open.md`, `closed.md`, `pipeline-log.md`, generated JSONs). `prospects/` holds raw consultation notes. `distillates/prospects/` holds their distillates. 49 references point at these paths, so they are documented rather than merged. |\\r\\n| Raw personal-data exports (Google Takeout, Gemini session transcripts) | `raw/data-about-me/` — unprocessed PII, git-ignored; never read wholesale |\\r\\n| Authoring content for students and audiences (notes, teaching material, writing in his voice, design) | `Cowork/` — the authoring lane. **Tool-agnostic despite the name**: Cowork is the default door, Claude Code works the same projects. One job per project folder; drafts stay, finished work lands in `students/` · `corpus/` · `wiki/`. No memory file of its own |\\r\\n| Design/plan thinking, dated (grill-me + interview outputs) | `brainstorms/` — supersedes the retired `aios/` reform folder |\\r\\n| Session handoffs · chat exports | `handoffs/` — operational records, deliberately OUTSIDE `raw/`; `session-handoff` writes here |\\r\\n| External teardowns and research, dated | `research/` |\\r\\n| Web-clipped articles (Obsidian Web Clipper) | `raw/Clippings/` — external source material, unprocessed; immutable like the rest of `raw/` |\\r\\n| Application code | `projects/lsatu/` — own repo, own instructions, own remote |\\r\\n| Other projects (life-path, …) | `projects/<name>/` — each with its own `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/` |\\r\\n\\r\\n## Retrieval order\\r\\n\\r\\n1. `wiki/hot.md` — current-session narrative and open threads.\\r\\n2. `wiki/index.md` → the page (teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md`; a student: their folder). Follow links; there is no semantic search and none is needed at this size.\\r\\n3. The live system — the final freshness escalation.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Distillate-first (session content):** when a task needs what happened in a tutoring session, read the distillate (`distillates/<student>/`) first; open the raw transcript (`students/<name>/transcript-downloads/`) only when the distillate is insufficient, and say you are escalating. Aggregate teaching questions (patterns across students, lecture prep) go to `corpus/Curriculum/<Topic>/_playbook.generated.md` first — it already joins the distillate excerpts by topic — and should not touch raw transcripts at all.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. On any disagreement, the backend wins. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Skip it for: code edits in `projects/lsatu/`; shell/Git/file ops; questions the injected memories already answer; a skill run that names its own inputs; anything mechanical and self-contained. Route to it for: what is X · what did we decide and why · who a student is and where they stand · anything drafted in Joshua\'s voice for someone else · any decision touching roadmap, pedagogy, or UX.\\r\\n\\r\\n**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.\\r\\n\\r\\n<!-- graphify:begin -->\\r\\n## Knowledge graph (graphify)\\r\\n\\r\\nA deterministic knowledge graph of this brain and the app lives under `graph/` (git-ignored, PII-derived, regenerable), unified into a global graph at `~/.graphify/global-graph.json`. It is an **optional retrieval aid, not a gate** — use it when it saves time; fall back to normal file reads freely (it can be stale).\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Query it** when a question is about how things connect: `graphify query \\"<question>\\" --graph ~/.graphify/global-graph.json`, `graphify path \\"A\\" \\"B\\"` for a relationship, `graphify explain \\"X\\"` for one concept, `graphify affected \\"X\\"` for \\"what depends on X\\". The `graphify` MCP server (in `.mcp.json`) exposes the same over tools.\\r\\n- **Subgraphs:** `graph/lsatu/` (app code — AST, free, refresh with `graphify update projects/lsatu`), `graph/brain/` (students/corpus/distillates prose — semantic pass, `--backend claude-cli`, rides the subscription).\\r\\n- **Backend is always `claude-cli`** (no API keys). Rebuild brain prose graphs with `graphify extract <path> --backend claude-cli`.\\r\\n- **Never point `graphify --wiki` at the real `wiki/`** — that mode deletes `wiki/*.md`. Graph-to-vault views go to `graph/obsidian-view/` via `to_obsidian` only.\\r\\n- **Codex/AGENTS.md is deliberately excluded** — graphify is a Claude-only skill in this brain (matches the \\"Codex runs the catalog minus wiki/Obsidian/graphify\\" rule). Do not mirror this section into AGENTS.md.\\r\\n<!-- graphify:end -->\\r\\n\\r\\n## Voice\\r\\n\\r\\n- **No em-dashes in anything a student or prospect will read, or in any draft/output for Joshua to send or paste.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust. This is the enforced rule; internal scratch/reference docs are not policed for them.\\r\\n- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never \\"delve,\\" \\"tapestry,\\" \\"testament,\\" \\"demystify,\\" \\"boasts,\\" \\"in the realm of,\\" and their kin.\\r\\n- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.\\r\\n- **Anything sent by EMAIL also routes through `wiki/email-voice.md`**, before the first draft and again before recording. New corrections are appended there as the next `E-NN`, written as the rule rather than the incident.\\r\\n- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don\'t over-hedge.\\r\\n- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Tools, keys, and secrets\\r\\n\\r\\n- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua\'s single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Joshua may hand a credential over in chat; take it and write it to `.env` without pushing back.\\r\\n- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** `.claude/settings.json` allow/deny lists + scoped API keys. Jay 2 acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.\\r\\n- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.\\r\\n- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/claude/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.\\r\\n- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.\\r\\n- **No Git remote**, ever, without first completing the scrub checklist in `wiki/HANDLING.md`. The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Skills\\r\\n\\r\\nEverything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.claude/skills/` (Codex: `.agents/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.\\r\\n\\r\\nActive in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:\\r\\n\\r\\n| Skill | Use it when |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua\'s explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |\\r\\n| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Claude↔Codex — chat-only, writes nothing |\\r\\n| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |\\r\\n| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |\\r\\n| `second-brain-ingest`, `-query`, `-lint` | filing into, asking of, or health-checking the wiki (the new-vault wizard `second-brain` is retired) |\\r\\n| `graphify` | turning any input into a knowledge graph |\\r\\n| `obsidian-cli`, `obsidian-markdown`, `obsidian-bases` | vault mechanics and graph traversal (`obsidian-vault` was deactivated to `_inactive/` and is no longer routable) |\\r\\n| `consultation-inquiry-drafts` (email + DM channels), `consultation-followup-drafts` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |\\r\\n| `lsatlabvideo` | turning one LSAT Lab video lesson into a Socratic session doc he can teach from (`/lsatlabvideo <url or videoid>`) |\\r\\n| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |\\r\\n| `session-tasks` | after a session: pulling out who owes what to which student by when, into `open-loops.md` + the task board. Joshua\'s to-do list, as distinct from `session-recap` (the student\'s document) and `wrapup` (the project ledger) |\\r\\n| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |\\r\\n| `pull-submissions` | pulling booking-time student uploads into `students/<name>/submissions/` |\\r\\n| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |\\r\\n| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |\\r\\n| `claudex-loop` | hardening a high-stakes plan before any code: recon -> interrogate -> Codex adversarial review -> optional build with cross-inspection. Supersedes `grill-me-codex` / `grill-with-docs-codex` (both retired 2026-08-28) |\\r\\n| `grill-with-docs` | stress-testing a plan **in a codebase with established terminology**: a two-line wrapper that runs `grilling` + `domain-modeling`. Writes a `CONTEXT.md` glossary (glossary and nothing else, never a spec) plus ADRs, and no brainstorm file. **Model-invocable since 2026-09-01** (Joshua\'s call): the `disable-model-invocation` lock upstream ships was removed, so \\"grill with docs\\" in conversation fires it. It is an **overlay**, not vendored, so the auto-updater will not put the lock back. **Never substitute `grill-me`**: that is exactly the 2026-09-01 failure, where the skill was inactive, the model could not reach it, and silently improvised a wrong-format glossary into a project `CONTEXT.md` |\\r\\n\\r\\n**When `/grill-with-docs` runs, confirm it actually loaded `grilling` and `domain-modeling`.** Upstream documents this as a real, unfixed rough edge: a skill that names another skill does not reliably cause it to load, and this one names two. The tell is a round that arrives with everything asked at once and no `➡️` recommendations attached, which is the model improvising an interview instead of running Matt\'s. Say so and load both explicitly rather than continuing. This is a second, independent way the skill can look like it ran and quietly do the wrong thing.\\r\\n| `grill-me` | stress-testing a plan by interview **when there is no codebase to be stateful about**. A thin **overlay** that calls `grilling` and adds two things upstream has not: a `brainstorms/{date}-{slug}.md` capture file checkpointed every round (Nate Herk\'s idea), and `AskUserQuestion` popups instead of plain text (Joshua\'s). Writes no ADRs or glossary. **All local additions live here and nowhere else.** Unlike upstream it is **deliberately model-invocable** — \\"grill me about X\\" mid-conversation should fire it, the way Nate demos it. Joshua\'s call, 2026-09-01; do not add `disable-model-invocation` |\\r\\n| `grilling` | the interview method itself (design tree, ask the whole frontier per round, never one question at a time). Loaded by the two above; rarely invoked directly. **Vendored verbatim — never edit it** |\\r\\n| `to-spec` | turning a finished grilling conversation into a spec. **This is upstream\'s answer to \\"where does everything that wasn\'t a term or an ADR go?\\"** — do not `/clear` after a grill, hand the same conversation to this. Replaces our retired `to-prd` + `to-issues`; `to-tickets` follows it |\\r\\n| `wayfinder` | planning an effort **too big for one session** — it charts the work as a map of decision tickets on the issue tracker and runs `grilling` inside each. The split is single-session `grill-with-docs` vs multi-session `wayfinder`. It plans, never builds. Needs tracker wiring, and falls back to local markdown without it |\\r\\n| `youtube-full` | anything where a video is the better source (transcripts, channel/topic research) |\\r\\n\\r\\n**Vendored upstream skills.** `grilling`, `grill-with-docs` and `domain-modeling` are Matt Pocock\'s ([github.com/mattpocock/skills](https://github.com/mattpocock/skills), MIT), kept **byte-identical to upstream** so they refresh by overwrite. Never edit a vendored file: local changes go in a separate overlay skill that calls it, which is what `grill-me` is. Declared in `system/upstream-skills.json`; `system/scripts/check-upstream-skills.py` reports drift (`--diff` to see it, `--apply` to take upstream). **Updates arrive on their own**: the `com.joshua.ai-os.upstream-skills` launchd agent runs Mondays and Thursdays at 09:00 and records every change in `wiki/upstream-updates.md`. Twice-weekly because upstream ships in bursts (median 5 days between update days across its last 10). It cannot damage an overlay — `--apply` refuses to write to one, aborts if the manifest ever lists one as vendored, and re-verifies the overlay\'s declared `must_contain` additions in all six roots afterwards. Before assuming a vendored skill is current, read that ledger: the batch sat 7 weeks stale once and sent a session to the wrong skill.\\r\\n\\r\\nWhen a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Codex runs the same set minus the wiki/Obsidian and `graphify` skills.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Guardrails\\r\\n\\r\\n- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.\\r\\n- **Root stays thin.** Don\'t accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.\\r\\n- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.\\r\\n- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`; it is blocked in the foreground. Use `run_in_background` plus `Monitor` for anything slow, and raise `timeout` explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the 2-minute default.\\r\\n- **Backtrack a bad retrieval, then fix the map.** When you miss something that was in the brain — you searched long for what should have been instant, or claimed you lacked access you had — do not just apologize and move on. Reconstruct where you actually looked, name where you should have looked, and then repair the cause: the routing line, the index entry, or the file\'s location. A retrieval failure is a defect in the map, not in the effort.\\r\\n- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don\'t grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.\\r\\n- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.\\r\\n- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.\\r\\n- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.\\r\\n- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU\'s recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua\'s explicit approval.\\r\\n- **Memory = native automemory.** \\"Remember that…\\" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.\\r\\n- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Dual-runtime (Claude + Codex)\\r\\n\\r\\nThis file is hand-edited and the edit sticks — change it directly (or ask Claude to). On any major change, **mirror it into `AGENTS.md`** (Codex\'s twin), which reads `AGENTS.md` and never this file. Everything that must hold for both runtimes lives in BOTH files; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them. Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → `/clear` → paste). Session cadence: at ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.\\r\\n\\r\\nActivation differs by runtime and nothing auto-loads: Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`, Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/` (agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with instructions in `developer_instructions`). The two sets are intentionally unequal — Codex runs the same catalog minus the wiki/Obsidian and `graphify` skills.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Agent skills\\r\\n\\r\\n### Issue tracker\\r\\n\\r\\nLocal markdown under `.scratch/<feature>/`; this repo has no remote. See `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Triage labels\\r\\n\\r\\nThe five canonical roles, unchanged. See `docs/agents/triage-labels.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Domain docs\\r\\n\\r\\nSingle-context: `CONTEXT.md` + `docs/adr/` at the root. See `docs/agents/domain.md`.\\r\\n"}'}]

## Tool: exec
